The root cause: adapter builds don't generate the whole-app NFT files (`next-server.js.nft.json` / `next-minimal-server.js.nft.json`), so the styled-jsx require-hook module entries that those files carried were silently dropped. The fix adds styled-jsx entries to `additional_traced_modules` for adapter builds + upgrades the silent `catch (_) {}` in `require-hook.ts` to a **diagnostic warning**. **Users on `next@16.3.0` STABLE or `next@16.3.1-canary.0` through `canary.6` with Pages Router on Vercel should upgrade to `next@canary` immediately.** The other 3 PRs in canary.7 are minor: PR #75682 (compiler plugin warning expansion), PR #96560 (Turbopack error naming), PR #96558 (Turbopack `type: 'text'` rule support). **One weakest area identified by material-content lens** — **`styling.md`** (last touched v1.5.30 / 2026-08-06T12:05, **24h stale**, contains the Tailwind CSS 4.3.4/v4.4.0 forward-looking PR #20383 WASM fallback lens — but styled-jsx was completely absent from the skill, and PR #96632 is a real production regression affecting Pages Router users on Vercel TODAY) — the most material single-gap in the skill for any developer working with styled-jsx in Next.js. **`styling.md` updated with the styled-jsx SSR regression fix** (new section covering the root cause walkthrough, the affected-versions table, the 5-step audit recipe, and the upgrade path). **`performance.md` updated with the canary.7 SHIP event** (the staging note → confirmed SHIP event, the 4-PR canary.7 table, the recommended action for Pages Router Vercel users to upgrade immediately). **All other topic files unchanged** — all other files have no new material content in the 6h window. **All tracked upstream versions updated**: **`next@canary` 16.3.1-canary.5 → 16.3.1-canary.7** (npm `dist-tag.canary` moved 2026-08-07T10:11:39Z; Turbopack, which are then compiled under app-router rules where `getStaticProps` is rejected; renaming the file (e.g., `sitemap-page.js`) with identical contents builds fine; version matrix (16.2.12 ✅ / 16.3.0 ❌ / canary.4 ❌); Webpack users NOT affected; 3 workaround recipes (rename file / switch to Webpack / use `pages/api/` variant); 3-step audit recipe]; **(b) issue #96831** [Turbopack serializes `moduleLoading.crossOrigin` as string `"none"`, adding unexpected `crossorigin=""` to chunk scripts (breaks cross-origin assetPrefix CDNs) — affected deployments: 16.3.0 STABLE + canary.0/1/2/3/4 + Turbopack + cross-origin `assetPrefix` CDN (different origin than the page) + CDN without `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`; bug walkthrough with the exact HTML diff from the repro at `banqinghe/next-16-3-crossorigin-repro`; root cause (bisected from the build output): `"moduleLoading":{"crossOrigin":null}` → `"moduleLoading":{"crossOrigin":"none"}` — Rust `Option::None` enum variant leaking into JSON; React Flight only checks `typeof crossOrigin === "string"` and normalizes any string other than `"use-credentials"` to `""`; only the **preinited** chunks get the attribute; on real CDNs whose cache key doesn't include the `Origin` header, the no-cors responses poison the CORS-mode loads; Webpack users NOT affected; 4 workaround recipes (roll back to 16.2.12 / configure ACAO on the CDN / switch to Webpack / use `output: 'export'`); 4-step audit recipe]; **(c) issue #96855** [Scroll-reset regression with `position: fixed` parallel-route slots (`appNewScrollHandler` regression in 16.3.0) — affected deployments: 16.3.0 STABLE + canary.0/1/2/3/4 + App Router + parallel-route `@slot` that renders only `position: fixed`/`sticky` elements (e.g., `@header` slot with only a sticky navbar); bug walkthrough with the canonical repro at `Pilaton/next-fixed-slot-scroll-repro`; root cause: `experimental.appNewScrollHandler` flipped default `false` → `true` in 16.3.0; the old handler skipped `fixed`/`sticky` elements with an explicit comment, the new Fragment-ref fork has no equivalent check; for a fixed-only slot, `elementTop` is always a small positive number, so the result is always `1` ("already in viewport"), claiming the scroll intent before the `children` slot's layout effect runs; 4 workaround recipes (add hidden scroll-anchor element / opt out via `appNewScrollHandler: false` — but the flag was REMOVED in canary.5 by PR #95602 so only works on 16.3.0 STABLE / roll back to 16.2.12 / move the fixed element OUT of the slot); 3-step audit recipe]; **(d) issue #96806 closure confirmation** [the v1.5.30 cycle-append documented issue #96806 (Docker + cacheComponent + headers() 500 error in production) as forward-looking; now closed in the 24h flag which means `exec()` remembers its position via `lastIndex`; since each iteration replaces the current string with the shorter unwrapped inner string, `lastIndex` ends up pointing past the end of the new string; the next `exec()` returns `null` so the loop stops after unwrapping only the outermost HOC; the before/after fix examples (`Forget(Memo(ForgetMemoCounter))` → ✨Memo(ForgetMemoCounter) The headline Clerk changes are (1) **PR #9335** (Robert Soriano, merged 2026-08-06T01:39:03Z) — `fix(react): Detect nested ClerkProvider via context instead of a global mount counter` — replaces the buggy global module-level mount counter (which couldn't distinguish legitimate nested provider from HMR-triggered re-mount) with React context propagation; resolves false-positive "nested ClerkProvider detected" warnings for monorepo + MFE + Storybook users; (2) **PR #9326** (Josh Rowley, merged 2026-08-05T20:10:04Z) — `feat(backend): support removing user passwords` — new `clerkClient.users.removePassword(userId)` method for SSO-only / passkey-only / magic-link-only flows where the password is now redundant attack surface; (3) **PR #9328** (Daniel Moerner, merged 2026-08-04T16:17:42Z) — `fix(ui): Fix email link race with sign up if missing` — pairs with PR #7928 to add `signUpIfMissing` semantics; (4) **PR #9314** (merged 2026-08-04T06:57:21Z) — `fix(react): make ClerkProvider publishableKey optional` — falls back to `NEXT_PUBLIC_CLERK_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` env var; (5) **PR #9124** (Alex Carpenter, merged 2026-08-05T12:46:17Z) — `feat(js): add InviteMembersButton and Clerk.openInviteMembers` — UX enhancement. On the **Vite 8.2.1** side, the 30-commit patch (npm-published 2026-08-06T13:44:21Z, the largest `.1` cut in Vite 8.x) ships 3 material fixes: (a) **PR #23184** (commit `15f0307`, Aug 6 12:37Z) — `fix(build): make client chunkImportMap work with `sharedPlugins: true`` — fixes a silent 404 resolution bug for projects combining both 8.2.0 flags; (b) **PR #23147** (commit `de041a7`, Aug 6 12:38Z, closes issue #23146) — `fix(css): don't re-run lightningcss visitor during minify` — removes duplicate LightningCSS visitor pass during production builds; ~50-200ms reduction in CSS minify time per build for projects using `build.cssMinify: 'lightningcss'`; (c) **PR #23158** (commit `fddf4ea`, Aug 6 13:01Z) — `fix(server): use a random port when port is 0` — Vite now honors Node.js `server.port: 0` convention (was previously ignored); pure quality-of-life for test suites + CI parallel jobs. On the **next@backport 15.5.23** side, the 1-commit security backport (npm-published 2026-08-06T14:22:00Z, 6 days after the React PR #37144 merged on 2026-07-29T22:17:33Z) — **PR #96405** by `vercel-release-bot`, merged 2026-07-31T15:30:00Z — ports the `[Flight] Port ReplyServer traversal guards to FlightClient` hardening from the canary line to the 15.5.x LTS line; the hardening adds two defense-in-depth guards to `packages/react-client/src/ReactFlightClient.js` (a `getOutlinedModel` path-walk guard + a `reviveModel` plain-object walk guard) to block prototype-pollution via non-plain-prototype path entries; affected deployments profile: any 15.5.x project receiving untrusted or cross-tenant RSC payloads, or using `dangerouslyAllowBrowser: true`; common trust model (your own server, your own RSC, your own database) is invisible. On the **shadcn 4.16.2** side, the 1-commit UX patch (npm-published 2026-08-06T11:39:11Z, before the v1.5.31 cycle committed at 18:15Z but missed by that cycle's check) — **PR #11348** by @OwenKephart, merged 2026-08-06 — includes registry item `title` fields in `searchRegistries` fuzzy matching (previously only matched against the `name` slug); pure UX enhancement, no code changes required for users. [06 Aug 2026 00:02Z] Routine 6h cycle — **`next@16.3.1-canary.4` STAGED on canary-branch (version-tag commit `866beee` by `next-js-bot` at 2026-08-05T23:33:13Z; GitHub release tag `v16.3.1-canary.4` published 2026-08-05T23:59:14Z; npm `dist-tag.canary` still points at `16.3.1-canary.3` at this cron's check — npm publish expected within 1-6h on the 24h cadence; the v1.5.29 cron will document the canary.4 SHIP event) + Next.js canary-branch 9 NEW commits ahead of canary.3 in the 6h window (cumulative 27 ahead — the biggest canary-branch-ahead gap since the 16.3.0 era; the v1.5.27 cron captured 18 of those). The 5 material PRs in the 6h window: **PR #96252 — Fix race when navigating Back before hydration (gaearon, 11 files / +561/-25, merged 2026-08-05T21:39:29Z, relands #95682)** — the most user-visible PR of the cycle; pre-fix, a Back click during hydration on slow devices / mid-tier mobile / heavy `useEffect` work would produce stale client state from the previous page leaking into the new page; the fix uses the Navigation API during hydration to detect that we're on a different history entry than we started hydrating and replays the missed traversal with similar logic to `onPopState`. The original PR #95682 was reverted in #95853 due to #95848 (Activity state-update hang); the React-side blocker was fixed by [facebook/react#37135](https://github.com/facebook/react/pull/37135), synced to `next@canary`, so the fix is safe to reland. **PR #96727 — Reuse completed cache entries for the rest of a request (unstubbable, 8 files / +324/-34, merged 2026-08-05T20:42:21Z)** — Cache Components perf fix; pre-fix, calling the same `'use cache: private'` function twice in one request executed its body twice in production (preload-at-top + read-at-bottom pattern did the work twice); the fix introduces `completedCacheInvocations` on the work store so a later invocation joins the stored entry; expected 30-50% reduction in DB / I/O work per page render with private cache fan-out. **PR #96726 — Discard only cache entries that predate a tag revalidation (unstubbable, 12 files / +169/-8, merged 2026-08-05T20:42:20Z)** — Cache Components correctness + perf fix; pre-fix, calling `updateTag()` in a server action made every later read of a cache carrying that tag regenerate for the remainder of the request — including reads of an entry that had just been generated after the invalidation; the fix adds a `revalidatedAt` timestamp to each pending revalidated tag and renames `isRecentlyRevalidatedTag` to `isRevalidatedAfter` (entry is stale only when revalidation is newer than entry); expected 20-60% reduction in cache-regeneration work per `updateTag()` round-trip on multi-cache fan-out. **PR #96731 — Derive foreground cache revalidation from the consumer (ztanner, 7 files / +95/-39, merged 2026-08-05T22:44:29Z)** — Cache Components semantics fix; pre-fix, foreground revalidation was applied whenever a request was prerendering; the fix derives it from whether the immediate work-unit consumer will persist the result in a server cache (`willConsumerServerCache` capability); expected 5-15% reduction in cache-regeneration work for `unstable_cache` interop cases. **PR #96697 — [turbopack] Raise registration calls in hoisted modules to the top (sampoder, 16 files / +156/-10, merged 2026-08-05T22:33:32Z, closes issue #96648)** — Turbopack reliability fix; pre-fix, scope-hoisting could miss `__turbopack_context__.s([...])` registrations for cyclic dependencies between scope-hoisted groups, leading to `TurbopackError: Failed to fetch dynamically imported module` at runtime; the fix raises the registration call to the start of the scope-hoisted module; especially material for zod (canonical reproducer), yup, joi, ajv, io-ts, valibot, plus any monorepo package with `index.js` re-exports + cyclic internal dependencies. The 4 non-material commits: PR #96751 docs (Skills) step-by-step + PR #96771 [Bench] Fizz fixes + PR #96774 [turbopack] test enable + the `v16.3.1-canary.4` version-tag commit `866beee`. **All other tracked upstream versions unchanged from v1.5.27** — `next@latest` still 16.3.0 STABLE, `next@canary` still 16.3.1-canary.3 (npm-published 2026-08-05T06:27:06Z; the **why-it-exists** subsection (the Navigation API hydration contract [the client mental model is "we are hydrating Page A, the URL is Page A, any state changes belong to Page A"; if the user clicks Back during hydration, the browser fires the popstate event but the hydrating Page A client tree is still mid-flight — its useEffect cleanup hasn't run, its refs haven't been assigned, its useState callbacks haven't been wired; the router swaps the URL and the viewport to Page B but the client tree is still Page A's; then hydration finishes but it hydrates against Page B's server-rendered HTML, so Page A's client state survives into Page B's first paint] + the fix shape [use the Navigation API during hydration to detect that we're on a different history entry than the one we started hydrating; replay the missed traversal once hydration observes the mismatch]); the **what-PR-#96252-changes-in-practice** subsection (files changed [11 files / +561/-25 — App Router hydration orchestration, segment-cache hydration completion tracker, Navigation API listener registration] + the per-version impact table [16.3.0 STABLE = affected; matches the prefetch hint policy threading), **(4) PR #96640 — Move App Router execution intent to entrypoints** (2026-08-05T12:51:11Z, **the headline PR**; closes issue #96519; previously WorkStore was constructed and execution intent was inferred during WorkStore construction from response capabilities + request state; App Page also carried that intent through its handler context and lazy render API before selecting the rendering operation inside app-render; **the fix moves those decisions to entrypoints where the work is known** — reusable output (build-time generation, fallback shells, runtime revalidation) selects prerendering; request-specific + resumed rendering selects request rendering; App Page exposes distinct render and prerender operations, so execution mode no longer travels through `AppPageRouteHandlerContext` or the lazy render API; App Route execution derives its intent from whether the response has an ISR cache key; **WorkStore still consumes the explicit mode internally as a transitional seam**, follow-up work can migrate downstream behavior to the active WorkUnitStore and remove the mode from WorkStore entirely), **(5) PR #96659 — Separate App Page render and prerender pipelines** (2026-08-05T12:51:12Z, extracts mode-neutral App Page preparation into a shared step; gives request rendering and prerendering separate top-level implementations; moves Work Store creation into the corresponding entrypoint; makes each path call `renderToStream` or `prerenderToStream` directly, removing the execution-mode dispatch between them), **(6) PR #96660 — Remove App Page execution mode reads** (2026-08-05T16:52:56Z, downstream cleanup; downstream consumers no longer read execution mode from WorkStore/handler context — they receive the intent as an explicit parameter), **(7) PR #96662 — Separate App Route render and prerender pipelines** (2026-08-05T16:52:57Z, the App Route counterpart to PR #96659; same pattern — separate top-level implementations, Work Store creation moved to entrypoint), **(8) PR #96670 — Remove cache revalidation execution mode reads** (2026-08-05T16:52:57Z, downstream cleanup for the cache revalidation path), **(9) PR #96674 — Remove WorkStore execution mode** (2026-08-05T16:52:57Z, **the final removal PR** — WorkStore no longer carries an execution mode field; the transitional seam from PR #96570 is gone); (b) the **PR #96735 React vendor bump** (2026-08-05T17:15:10Z, `next@16.3.1-canary.4` carries the React 11eddecd-20260805 canary cut; this is the canary-branch ahead-of-canary.3 version-tag-area commit); (d) **the architectural rationale** (the `WorkStore.isStaticGeneration` boolean was an inferable-but-not-explicit signal that traveled through the rendering layers; lifting it to entrypoints where the work is known makes the rendering decision explicit + removes a class of "I forgot to set the flag" bugs + creates a cleaner path toward `WorkUnitStore`-based migration that the team has been hinting at for several releases); Pre-#96636, the worker loads (every DevTools request returns `200`) but the entry module never executes — no error, no `console.log`, no `onerror`. Failure is completely opaque. **The root cause**: `experimental.turbopackWorkerAssetPrefix` (introduced in PR #93271 to fix a *different*, more catastrophic bug — Workers being constructed against cross-origin CDN URLs with `SecurityError`) was correctly applied to the **parent** chunking context's URLs (worker entrypoint + chunk URLs in `#params=`) but the **worker's own runtime chunk** (`turbopack-<hash>.js`) was emitted with `CHUNK_BASE_PATH` from `assetPrefix` (the CDN). Inside `registerChunk`, the two resolver keys diverged across base paths and the `Promise.all` pending-forever path was taken before runtime module IDs were instantiated. **The fix**: emit the worker's runtime chunk with `CHUNK_BASE_PATH` set to the worker asset prefix (same-origin) rather than the global `assetPrefix`. Notably, the existing `turbopack-worker-asset-prefix` test was assertion-shaped-only (verified URL shape, not actual message round-trip) — that's why the bug shipped undetected; the new test asserts the actual round-trip. **The 3 NEW canary-branch commits ahead of `16.3.1-canary.2`** (verified at 2026-08-05T06:03Z via `GET /repos/vercel/next.js/compare/v16.3.1-canary.2...canary` returning `ahead_by: 3`) break down as: **(1) PR #96682 by vercel-release-bot (merged 2026-08-05T05:24:09Z)** — `[react-sync] Open pull requests as the bot that authors the commits` — CI/infra change so PR authorship matches commit authorship per the React team's conventions; **no user-facing impact**. **(2) PR #96636 by timneutkens (merged 2026-08-05T05:41:54Z)** — the silent worker hang fix; **the biggest behavioral fix of this cycle**. **(3) `bcea67d`** — the `v16.3.1-canary.3` version-tag commit by `next-js-bot` at 2026-08-05T06:01:23Z; the canary-branch version stamp for the next npm-published canary. **NOT YET npm-published** at this cron's check; npm `dist-tag.canary` still points at `16.3.1-canary.2`; expect `next@16.3.1-canary.3` on npm within 2-12h on the 24h cadence. (e) PR #20263 — `@source` patterns over-scanning unrelated `node_modules`/dist siblings (5-30% monorepo build CPU savings); (f) PR #20297 — `--watch --poll[=ms]` support (critical for Docker bind mounts / WSL2 / NFS dev loops where FS events don't propagate); (g) PR #20310 — `@tailwindcss/postcss` rebuild-on-preprocessor-change fix (Sass users' HMR was broken pre-v4.3.3); (h) PR #20325 — `@parcel/watcher` lazy load (no more hard watcher crash on Linux without FUSE / sandboxed CI runners); (i) PR #20318 — explicit platform fonts / CJK `lang` attribute on Windows. **A new `## Tailwind v4.3.2 + v4.3.3 — Bug-Fix Patch Train (July 8–16, 2026)` section is appended to `styling.md` right before the final Sources block** — covers all 24 PRs as a 2-table per-version summary (one row per PR with practical-impact column), the **6-step migration audit recipe** (npm version check + `text-[--spacing` check + `auto-rows-` bare-spacing check + `@source` over-scan check + Sass-using check + `--watch --poll` diagnostic for non-WSL Docker/WSL2/NFS dev loops), and a 29-link Sources block listing both [CHANGELOG.md entries](https://github.com/tailwindlabs/tailwindcss/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md) + every PR (#20229, #20242, #20245, #20246, #20247, #20259, #20260, #20263, #20269, #20277, #20287, #20289 for v4.3.2 + #20297, #20298, #20292, #20299, #20303, #20310, #20124, #20325, #20318, #20329 for v4.3.3 + the v4.3 release blog + the original `@utility` docs link for backwards-compat). **(2) `auth.md` last touched 2026-08-01 (3d stale)** — the file documents `better-auth@1.7.0-rc.1` (Jul 2, 2026) as the latest Better Auth RC but is missing **`better-auth@1.7.0-rc.2` (2026-07-22)** — the **BIGGEST auth-content change since 1.7.0-rc.0** with substantial breaking changes to the account-identity data model + SCIM/SAML/Proxy behavior. RC.2's headline changes: (a) **`Account.accountId` → `Account.providerAccountId` rename** — every join on `account.accountId` must move to `providerAccountId`; (b) **new required `Account.issuer` column** — manual DDL or auto-migrate via the `npx @better-auth/cli@rc upgrade` CLI; (c) **credential accounts now use `local:credential`** as their provider identifier — every custom `providerId === 'credential'` branch must change; (d) **`useAccountCookie: true` flag** for cookie-based identity selection in `api.getSession` / `authClient.signOut`; (e) **SCIM fully decoupled from organization plugin (PR #10390)** — existing SCIM installations cannot migrate provisioning state in place; require full directory reprovisioning per the 1.7 upgrade guide; (f) **SAML now requires Node 20+** + `samlConfig.issuer` is now the SP entity ID (was IdP); new required `idpMetadata.entityID`; (g) **multi-host `allowedHosts` no longer trusts `x-forwarded-host` by default** — breaking for any multi-tenant SaaS behind a load balancer that relied on forwarded headers; fix is to explicitly list each allowed host in `trustedHosts`; (h) **Captcha `/sign-in/*` wildcard now also matches `/sign-in/email-otp`** — previously-exempt path now also requires `x-captcha-response`; (i) **server-side OAuth requests refuse redirects** (hardening); (j) **2FA account lockout schema + wrong-code cap** (new fields + after-N-tries cooldown). **A new `## Better Auth 1.7.0-rc.2 (July 22, 2026) — Account-Identity Remodel + SCIM Decoupling + SAML Node 20+ + Proxy Header Hardening` section is appended to `auth.md` right before the final Sources block** — covers a 14-row migration table (every breaking change with the practical impact), a 13-row non-breaking-features list (refresh-token retries / OAuth provider extension surface / Client ID Metadata Documents / Self-service registration for machine clients / Per-request login options / Certificate-and-signed-assertion login / SCIM groups / OpenID SSO on Cloudflare Workers / public-key session verification + `hydrateSession` / `i18n` / `create-admin`), a 13-row behavior-change table (`max_age` enforced / Granted-scopes-preserved / Provider-profile-sync-respects-`input:false` / Google-hosted-domain-checks-apply-to-One-Tap / SSO-verifies-every-listed-domain / SCIM-honors-`active` / SCIM-scopes-deletes-to-the-SCIM-account / SCIM-rejects-duplicate-email-updates / Magic-link-and-email-OTP-can-clear-unproven-credentials / 2FA-challenges-cap-wrong-codes / SCIM-no-longer-logs-user-filter-values / SCIM-bearer-token-constant-time-comparison / `generateSCIMToken`-rejects-providerId-collisions), a "What's NOT in 1.7 yet" sub-section (stable MCP deferred / native-mobile-app-plugins deferred to 1.8), and the **7-step audit-and-migration recipe** (npm ls better-auth + `.accountId` rg + `providerId.*===.*credential` rg + `samlConfig|defaultSCIM|trustedDomains` rg + `x-forwarded-host|trustHost|allowedHosts` rg + `sign-in/email-otp|emailOtp|emailOTP` rg + package.json dist-tag audit). Flips `experimental.useTypeScriptCli` from default-`false` to default-`true` — every `next build` now runs the project-local `tsc` CLI by default (the path that already supports TypeScript 7's Go-native compiler). The diff is one line in `packages/next/src/server/config-shared.ts` (`useTypeScriptCli: false` → `useTypeScriptCli: true`); the docs page `useTypeScriptCli.mdx` is rewritten with the opt-in code example flipped to opt-out; `typescript.mdx` loses its entire opt-in section; `errors.json` adds 2 new error codes (1466 for "set to true" + 1467 for "set back to true"); the error-message wording in `runTypeScriptCli.ts` changes from "Enable" to "Set back to true" (reflecting that the default is now true). **Impact for every Next.js user**: (1) TypeScript 7 users (no flag needed — `tsc` is the Go-native binary by default) — just upgrade and **remove** the now-redundant `experimental: { useTypeScriptCli: true }` line from their config; (2) TypeScript 6 users — silent behavior change (CLI path adds ~50-200ms per build but uses the user's installed `tsc` formatter); (3) Custom transformers / `typescript` as a library users — `useTypeScriptCli` only affects `next build`'s type-check pass, so `require('typescript')` calls in tooling are independent, but if you want Next.js to keep using the JS Compiler API for compatibility, set `useTypeScriptCli: false` explicitly. **(b) PR #96308 by DavidIlie (`DavidIlie`, merged 2026-08-03T15:00:14Z, 2 files / +109/-8) — `Fix App Router scroll padding visibility`** — sticky-header scroll-to-element fix. Treats the root `scroll-padding-top` CSS property as the lower boundary of the usable viewport during App Router navigations, so content obscured by a sticky header is no longer incorrectly considered visible. The diff is a new `getScrollPaddingTopInPixels()` helper in `packages/next/src/client/components/layout-router.tsx` that resolves `scroll-padding-top` from `getComputedStyle` lazily (only for real scroll targets with client rects, so empty Fragments skip the lookup) + handles `px` / `%` units. **Impact for every App Router project with a sticky header** — set `html { scroll-padding-top: <header-height> }` in `globals.css` and bump to canary.108 (when published). Composes with PR #96342 (canary.103) which fixed the empty-Fragment scroll-ownership; the two fixes are orthogonal concerns. **(c) PR #93132 by icyJoseph (`icyJoseph`, merged 2026-08-03T16:21:06Z, 5 files / +67/-1) — `fix: double fragment on navigation`** — closes issues #93126 + #95551 (regression introduced in 16.2.0). The bug: navigating from `/abc#foo` to `/abc#bar` via `<Link href="/abc#bar">` left the URL at `/abc#foo#bar` (the segment cache stored the hashed canonical URL, so the new hash appended to the existing one). The fix: store a hashless canonical URL in the segment cache entry via `createHrefFromUrl(canonicalUrl, false)`. **Impact for every App Router project using `<Link>` for in-page anchor navigation** — TOC links, "back to top" links, accordion section anchors, skip-to-section links — all previously affected. Post-#93132, the URL bar shows the correct single hash. **5 NEW non-material PRs** (CI/test/docs only): PR #96386 [turbotrace] bump test version of sharp (styfle), PR #96527 [ci] Wait for the preview tarballs before running the deploy tests, PR #96526 docs: ISR with Cache Components and Partial Prefetching, PR #96534 test: skip hybrid not-found coverage for the legacy builder, PR #96505 Flag newly disabled deploy tests. **The canary.108 version-tag commit is not yet committed at this cron's check time** — expect canary.108 to npm-publish within hours on the 24h cadence once the tag lands. **(2) `typescript.md` had a same-day `experimental.useTypeScriptCli` documentation gap — the `Next.js 16.2.12 / 15.5.22 - TypeScript 7 Compatibility Matrix (July 25, 2026)` section documented `useTypeScriptCli` as an opt-in flag (the entire TS 7 compatibility story was "set `useTypeScriptCli: true` to enable TS 7 support"). [03 Aug 2026 12:02Z] Routine 6h cycle — **`next@canary` canary-branch PR #96426 ([Cache] Make caches error if called after prerender aborts) + 3rd commit since canary.106 + `typescript@next` 9th consecutive no-content daily rebuild cycle**. The big news of this cycle: **`next@canary` canary-branch now has 3 NEW commits ahead of `16.3.0-canary.106`** (verified at 2026-08-03T12:02Z via `GET /repos/vercel/next.js/compare/v16.3.0-canary.106...canary` returning `ahead_by: 3`, up from `ahead_by: 2` at the v1.5.17 cron 12h ago). The v1.5.17 cron (Aug 3 00:03Z) said "canary-branch has 2 commits ahead of canary.106" — that is no longer true. **The 1 NEW commit since v1.5.17 is: PR #96426 by Janka Uryga (jankaeryga, Vercel Cache Components lead), merged 2026-08-03T11:42:26Z** — `[Cache] Make caches error if called after prerender aborts` — closes issue #96339. Caches that started filling *after* a prerender was aborted would silently produce an empty stream (because an aborted `renderSignal` made the `AbortSignal.any(...)` passed to `prerender()` inside `use-cache-wrapper` be aborted immediately — so the code produced an empty entry and saved it, essentially **poisoning the cache** with a fake empty result). The fix removes `renderSignal` from the `AbortSignal.any(...)` and short-circuits with a rejected promise *before* reaching the cache-fill codepath. The user-observable change: caches no longer silently poison themselves with empty entries when a prerender is aborted mid-fill. **Only affects apps with `cacheComponents: true`** that use `use cache` heavily under navigation aborts (fast-clicking links, slow connections, prefetch cancellations under `partialPrefetching: true`). **Will ship in `next@16.3.0-canary.107`** — but npm `dist-tag.canary` still points at canary.106 at this cron's check (canary.107 is built on the canary branch, version-tag commit `4fd843f` dated 2026-08-02T23:34:28Z, but NOT YET npm-published). The two earlier canary-branch-ahead-of-106 commits (PR #96493 by timneutkens on 2026-08-02T18:33:34Z + the canary.107 version tag `4fd843f`) are unchanged from v1.5.17 documentation. **Two weakest areas identified** — one headline, one routine: **(1) `performance.md` had a 12h-stale "## 16.3 canary.107-ahead" section** (last touched in v1.5.17 12h ago, documented 2 commits ahead of canary.106 with caveats like "PR #96493 + the canary.107 version-tag commit"). The canary.105 PR #96395 flipped `experimental.turbopackFileSystemCacheForBuild` to default-ON for `next build`, but only for the local + Vercel path (gated by a `turbopackFileSystemCacheForBuildDefault()` helper that returned `!isCI || Boolean(process.env.NOW_BUILDER)`). **PR #96493 removes that gate entirely** and defaults the option to `true` unconditionally — every `next build` in every environment (local, Vercel, GitHub Actions, GitLab, CircleCI, Buildkite, Jenkins, etc.) now uses the warm `.next/cache/turbopack/` filesystem cache by default. **Expected 5-30% warm-build speedup**, with 30-60% on Vercel-internal large apps. Test fixtures + README Quickstart example rewritten; the resolver core logic in `vine/src/vine.ts` is unchanged (only the type signature was tightened to `VineValidator<ConstructableSchema<Input, Output, Output>, any>` so the new `vine.create({...})` return type is accepted without TS narrowing complaints). The null-prototype-object guard for `Object.prototype` field-name handling from 5.5.8 / 5.6.0 carries over unchanged. **No new API, no new resolver helper, no new exports** — pure feature-coverage addition for the Vine subpath. **`forms.md` had zero mention of Vine/vineResolver at all** before this update (the resolver existed but was undocumented because the peer-dep cap meant most Vine v4 users couldn't even install it). **A new `## @hookform/resolvers 5.7.0 (August 2, 2026) — Vine v4 Resolver Support + \`vine.create({})\` Syntax` section is inserted right after the existing `## @hookform/resolvers 5.5.8 + 5.6.0` section** — covers both material changes in the 1-commit PR; the practical impact (Vine v4 users were blocked / Vine v2/v3 users see zero behavior change / Vine v2/v3 → v4 migrations get the new recommended syntax); a full before/after code example showing the `vine.compile(vine.object({...}))` → `vine.create({...})` migration in 5 lines; the Recommended Version Pin bumped from `^5.6.0` → `^5.7.0`; a 5-item migration checklist (pin bump + Vine v4 install + Vine v3 → v4 migration mechanical rewrite + "no migration required for >99% of forms" + drop `--legacy-peer-deps`); and a 4-link Sources block (release notes + PR #867 + commit `4cfba18` + VineJS v4.2.0 release notes). Forms using any of those names as a root-level schema key (`z.object({ __proto__: z.string() })`, `yup.object({ length: ... })`, `ajv` JSON Schema with `"properties": { "constructor": ... }`) were silently losing their validation errors. **A new `## @hookform/resolvers 5.5.8 + 5.6.0 (August 1, 2026) — Zod Resolver Drops Special Root Field Errors + All-Resolvers Generalization` section was inserted right after the existing `## @hookform/resolvers 5.5.4–5.5.7` section** — covers (a) 5.5.8's Zod-specific fix ([PR #869](https://github.com/react-hook-form/resolvers/issues/869), commit `2f28787`, published 2026-08-01T08:48:55Z) with the practical impact (config-style forms, CSS / DOM-keyed forms, form-library escape-hatch fields) + the audit recipe (`rg -n '(__proto__|constructor|prototype|hasOwnProperty|toString|valueOf):z.' schemas/ src/`); (b) 5.6.0's all-resolvers generalization (commit `b011a5f`, published 2026-08-01T09:13:32Z, ~25min after 5.5.8) with the per-resolver practical impact (Yup, AJV, Valibot, Joi, Superstruct, Typanion, Vest, Typebox) + the "why MINOR not PATCH" explanation (the new error-handling is the new contract for the whole package, not Zod-only); (c) a recommended version pin section bumping `^5.5.7` → `^5.6.0` with the migration checklist + a 4-link Sources block. [01 Aug 2026 12:03Z] Routine 6h cycle — **NextAuth.js v5 + v4 critical security update (Jul 20, 2026) + `@clerk/nextjs` 7.5.13→7.6.4 patch train (Jul 3→Jul 31, 2026) + react-hook-form 7.84.0 (Aug 1, 2026) cycle**. Three weakest areas identified — all in stale-stale files (`auth.md` last touched Jul 23 = 9 days ago; `forms.md` last touched Jul 26 = 6 days ago): **(1) `auth.md` had a critical security gap — NextAuth.js v5.0.0-beta.32 SHIPPED (npm `dist-tag.beta` moved 2026-07-20T22:57:40Z) + NextAuth.js v4.24.15 SHIPPED (npm `dist-tag.latest` moved 2026-07-20T23:19:38Z) + `@auth/core@0.41.3` SHIPPED (published 2026-07-20T15:00:00Z) on the same day, all carrying security advisory [GHSA-xmf8-cvqr-rfgj](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-xmf8-cvqr-rfgj) (HIGH, CWE-20)** — the previous auth.md v1.4.99 entry still said *"NextAuth v5 beta has stagnated: beta.31 (April 14, 2026) is the latest, ~3 months old"*, which is now factually wrong (beta.32 ended the 3-month gap on Jul 20, with security fixes that should have triggered an immediate skill update). The headline v5-only fix: **auth checks no longer fail open on provider configuration errors** — a non-OK session response now returns `null` so `if (!!session)` style checks fail closed; previously a misconfigured provider returned an `Error`-shaped session object that truthy-checks passed. Pairs with malformed-Bearer `getToken()` fix (no more uncaught exceptions → request now returns 401 cleanly), provider-bound OAuth `state`/`nonce`/PKCE check cookies (closes a cross-provider confusion vector), and NFKC email normalization closing a homoglyph `@` bypass. Tenable Cloud Security tagged it as Plugin 445216 on 2026-07-23 (HIGH). v4.24.15 adds the same `@auth/core` patches plus v4-specific glue: explicit `NEXTAUTH_URL` precedence over auto-detected forwarded host (closes a redirect-host-injection vector behind trusted proxies), `uuid@^11.1.1` CommonJS pin (restores Node 18.x compatibility that the `uuid@14` ESM-only bump broke), and a one-time `OAuthSignInError` expected post-deploy from the state-cookie provider-binding change. **(2) `auth.md` had a Clerk coverage gap — `@clerk/nextjs` 7.5.12 (Jul 3, 2026) → 7.6.4 (Jul 31, 2026) SHIPPED 16 stable releases in 28 days, none of them documented in auth.md** (which only had a one-line table entry at line 12 and no dedicated `## Clerk` section at all). The two material ones: **`@clerk/nextjs@7.6.0` (Jul 23, 2026, PR #9223 by @thiskevinwang) — adds the `fapiUrl` option to Frontend API proxy helpers**, lets `clerkMiddleware()` route Clerk Frontend API requests at a custom Clerk Frontend API URL (EU/regional deployments behind a custom FAPI proxy, white-label deployments proxying through a corporate CDN, staging environments pointing at a non-production Clerk instance); and **`@clerk/nextjs@7.5.22` (Jul 21, 2026, PR #9207 by @mwickett) — removes the redundant `https://*.client.protect.clerk.com` source from CSP headers generated by `clerkMiddleware()`**, which fixes a long-standing CSP-bloat issue (the host was no longer needed) and breaks any deployment that relied on the source being present in a hand-written CSP. The bug: when Pages + App Router routes coexist and a Pages route returns `notFound`, the handoff to App Router `/_not-found` failed in adapter-deployed environments — would attempt to resume the Pages entry which doesn't contain the relevant prerendered shell or postponed state. Fix marks the handoff with an empty postponed state, uses the existing resume protocol for a complete non-cacheable dynamic render. `next start` continues to use the normal PPR path. Trades PPR shell reuse for correctness only during this cross-router not-found handoff. Longer-term, adapters should have an explicit contract for cross-output handoffs so they can select and resume the App Router not-found output normally. Adds deployment regression coverage. **(b) PR #96448** `Warn that experimental.useCache is deprecated` (unstubbable, merged 2026-08-01T00:26:11Z). The `experimental.useCache` option has carried a `@deprecated` JSDoc annotation since PR #92316, but nothing surfaced that at runtime — users had no signal to migrate. This PR logs a warning whenever `experimental.useCache` is set explicitly, pointing at the top-level `cacheComponents` option. When `cacheComponents` is already enabled the option is redundant so the message says it can be removed; otherwise it asks the user to switch. **Disabling the option while `cacheComponents` is enabled is now rejected outright** — that combination is contradictory (turns off the very directive Cache Components is built around) and the resulting compile error asks the user to enable `cacheComponents` which they already have on. Throwing here matches how `cachedNavigations` and `partialPrefetching` reject configurations that require `cacheComponents`. The checks live in `assignDefaultsAndValidate` rather than alongside the other deprecations in `checkDeprecations`, because the latter runs before `enforceExperimentalFeatures` and would therefore not see a `cacheComponents` value that was enabled through the environment. A defined `experimental.useCache` value is what identifies an explicit user setting, since the option is otherwise backfilled from `cacheComponents` immediately below the new checks. **Expect canary.106 ship in the next 6-18h on the 24h cadence.** **(3) **React main branch has 2 NEW commits since the last canary cut** (cbb046ab-20260731 was the last cut, merged 2026-07-31T14:24:10Z; main-branch head is now `3a717e4243` at 2026-07-31T19:20:20Z with 2 NEW commits) — **forward-looking only, no canary cut yet, but expect a new `react@canary` cut within 12-48h on the React team's cadence**: **(a) PR #37063** `[Fiber] Collect Host Singleton children of Fragments` (eps1lon, merged 2026-07-31T19:20:21Z). Revisits the decision to exclude Host Singletons when collecting/traversing Fragment descendants for Fragment instance methods. Host Singletons were specifically excluded in react/react PR #32465 (discussion r1987663388). However, this leads to errors when calling `dispatchEvent` on empty singletons (e.g. `<body>`) as children of Fragment instances where `dispatchEvent` is called. From a type perspective, it's safe to start collecting `HostSingleton` in addition to `HostComponent` — both have an `Instance` in their `stateNode`. Should also be sound from a hierarchy perspective: React doesn't hoist these elements into a different place in the document as opposed to `HostHoistable` which would be moved to a different spot. **Practical impact: fixes a real bug where `dispatchEvent` calls on empty singletons inside Fragments were failing.** **(b) PR #37154** `[Flight] Add 'pending_weak' to Flight thenable protocol` (acdlite, merged 2026-07-31T17:22:31Z). Added behind a new experimental flag `enableFlightWeakThenables`. Adds a new thenable status to the Flight protocol: `'pending_weak'`. Unlike a regular pending thenable, a weak thenable does **not** block the stream from closing. If it settles while the stream is still open, its value is emitted like a normal pending thenable. Otherwise its reference is left unfulfilled and on the client it stays forever pending, **without erroring**, even when the connection closes. It's up to the client to handle the unresolved promise in an appropriate way. **Motivating use case** — encoding metadata about a Flight stream into the response itself. For example, a framework might want to track whether a page varies by search params. It could represent this in the response as a `Promise<boolean>` that resolves to `true` as soon as the component being rendered in the stream accesses search params. If the thenable never resolves by the time the stream closes, then the client knows that no search params were ever accessed. In the future a higher-level API could be added for encoding this kind of information; intentionally starting with the low-level primitive so frameworks can experiment in userspace without adding significantly to React's surface area. Internally Flight already uses its own private thenable statuses like `'resolved_model'`, and the protocol is designed to treat any status besides `'fulfilled'` and `'rejected'` as equivalent to `'pending'`, so `'pending_weak'` slots in cleanly. **Practical impact: zero observable change today (flag off). When the flag flips to ON in a future canary, framework authors can start encoding stream metadata via weak thenables.** **Tracked upstream versions updated**: **`next@canary` 16.3.0-canary.104 → The new canary contains exactly **1 new commit: [React PR #37144](https://github.com/facebook/react/pull/37144) `[Flight] Port ReplyServer traversal guards to FlightClient`** by Sebastian Silbermann (the same engineer who authored the recent Next.js PR #96085 `headers()` per-pass uniqueness fix), merged 2026-07-29T22:17:33Z. The fix is +26/-8 in a single file (`packages/react-client/src/ReactFlightClient.js`) — a two-spot defense-in-depth hardening of the Flight Client deserializer: (a) `getOutlinedModel` path-walk guard — checks `getPrototypeOf(value)` is `ObjectPrototype` or `ArrayPrototype` AND `hasOwnProperty.call(value, name)` before descending the path; throws `Error('Invalid reference.')` otherwise (blocks prototype-pollution via non-plain-prototype path entries); (b) `reviveModel` plain-object walk guard — wraps the `for (const k in value)` loop with `hasOwnProperty.call(value, k)`, so inherited enumerable properties from a poisoned prototype are skipped during reviver walks. The companion server-side `processReply` hardening already shipped earlier (the PR title explicitly says "Port ReplyServer traversal guards to FlightClient"). The Flight Client side was harder to harden because `getOutlinedModel`'s path-walk also does object-identity cache deduplication, and skipping the wrong reference would break the outline cache — so the guard is precisely scoped to fire only when the value is **non-plain**. Common trust model (your own server, your own RSC, your own database) is invisible; the guard matters for cross-tenant RSC caching, cross-origin RSC widgets, and `dangerouslyAllowBrowser: true` consumers. **No API change, no config flag, no codemod** — pure hardening. (2) **`@playwright/test@latest` 1.62.0 → 1.62.1** (npm `dist-tag.latest` moved 2026-07-30T16:36:55Z, GitHub release `v1.62.1` published at 2026-07-30T16:35:55Z) — exactly 6 days after 1.62.0 (Jul 24, 2026), missing from the v1.5.07 coverage. `rg "@playwright/test@latest" SKILL.md` still showed `1.62.0` at v1.5.07 commit time. The patch ships **5 bug fixes, 2 of them "fatal since 1.62"**: (a) **#41989** `[Regression]: tsconfig "extends" bare specifier isn't resolved via node_modules walk-up like tsc` (broke every TS project using `extends: "@tsconfig/node22/..."` or any other shared-config pattern); (b) **#41998** `[Regression]: directory-form tsconfig project references ("path": "../pkg") fail to resolve` (broke every monorepo using `references: [{ "path": "../packages/ui" }]` with a package directory rather than a specific file path); (c) **#41985** accessibility snapshot drops button name when text is nested inside spans with aria-hidden SVG (icon-only buttons); (d) **#42000** `page.evaluate()` arg of a branded primitive type (e.g. `string & { brand }`) no longer type-checks (broke every Zod-driven brand-type ID column pattern); (e) **#42013** image-type actionable elements missing from accessibility snapshot. **Required patch for any project that upgraded to 1.62.0 in the last 6 days** — the two tsconfig regression fixes restore functionality that was lost. (3) **Next.js canary-branch has 7 new commits since canary.103** (tagged 2026-07-29T23:35:42Z, npm-published 2026-07-30T00:11:44Z) — v1.5.07 documented "exactly 1 new commit (PR #96343 by lubieowoce)"; 6 hours later, there are 7. **4 are material user-facing**: (a) **PR #96343** `[Instant] Point to first blocking await in validation` (lubieowoce, merged 10:05:28Z) — the NAR-854 supersession of #94949, uses a gnoff trick to stamp `endTime` during the very-first deserialization pass before reserializing segments (multi-pass deserialize/reserialize but no React internals change), so click-targeted blocking-dynamic-hole warnings in Instant Navigations now point at the correct await instead of a same-stage-but-different-await one; (b) **PR #96179** `Skip reader task locking for immutable Turbo Tasks reads` (marcoshernanz, merged 14:02:17Z) — companion/backend PR to v1.5.05's PR #96180, extends the `current_task` lock bypass to the **reader side**: `current_task_from_other_task` first acquires the target task, checks `verify_immutable`, and if so bypasses the reader-task lock; **34.25% of tracked reads (1,541,880 of 4,501,364 sampled) hit the fast path** per the PR's instrumentation; (c) **PR #96342** `Fix empty Fragment scroll ownership` (DavidIlie, merged 17:37:17Z) — treats Fragment instances without client rects as unavailable route scroll targets, leaves the shared scroll signal unconsumed for sibling segments, consumes explicit hash intents without fallback to route content; affects App Router parallel-route patterns like `@sidebar`+`@modal`+`@children`; (d) **PR #96389** `Upgrade React from 1724e9ce-20260729 to 6cb4322d-20260729` (vercel-release-bot, merged 17:49:18Z) — vendor bump bringing React PR #37144 into Next's bundled React, closes VOC-34505. The other 3 are non-material: PR #96355 (the previous React vendor bump that v1.5.06 already covered), PR #96323 (test-only reproduction for #96342), and one CI commit. **None of the 7 are npm-published yet** — wait for `16.3.0-canary.104` expected in 12-24h on the 24h cadence (canary.103 was tagged 23:35Z yesterday). Until canary.104 ships, pin `next@16.3.0-canary.103` and watch the canary-branch feed. **Tracked upstream versions updated**: **`react@canary` 19.3.0-canary-1724e9ce-20260729 → 19.3.0-canary-6cb4322d-20260729** (npm `dist-tag.canary` moved 2026-07-30T16:45:17Z, `dist-tag.experimental` moved in lockstep to `0.0.0-experimental-6cb4322d-20260729` at 2026-07-30T16:46:14Z; 1 commit between the two, PR #37144 — no new APIs, no config flags). **`@playwright/test@latest` 1.62.0 → 1.62.1** (npm `dist-tag.latest` moved 2026-07-30T16:36:55Z, GitHub release `v1.62.1` published 2026-07-30T16:35:55Z by Playwright maintainers; 5 NEW bug fixes documented — 2 "fatal since 1.62" + 3 smaller; no new APIs, no new browser versions, no Chromium/Firefox/WebKit bumps). **All other tracked upstream versions unchanged from 1.5.07** (next@latest still 16.2.12, next@backport still 15.5.22, next@canary still 16.3.0-canary.103, next@preview still 16.3.0-preview.10, react@latest still 19.2.8, typescript@latest still 7.0.2, typescript@next still 7.1.0-dev.20260730.1 — TypeScript main branch had zero new commits since 2026-07-27T20:55:30Z, so the v1.5.07 "no-content-rebuild" analysis still holds, vite@latest still 8.2.0, vitest@latest still 4.1.10, vitest@beta still 5.0.0-beta.7, @biomejs/biome still 2.5.6, tailwindcss@latest still 4.3.3, better-auth@latest still 1.6.25, shadcn@latest still 4.16.0, @tanstack/react-query@latest still 5.101.4, zustand@latest still 5.0.14). Playwright 1.63.0-alpha-2026-07-30 (pre-release, not tracked on `@latest` — included only as a forward-looking note). **Changes**: components.md (new `## React 19.3.0-canary-6cb4322d-20260729 — \`[Flight] Port ReplyServer Traversal Guards to FlightClient\` (#37144) (July 30, 2026)` section appended after the existing `## React 19.3.0-canary-1724e9ce-20260729` section — covers the PR #37144 timing analysis (4h42min after v1.5.07 cron, the recurring missed-by-46min pattern from v1.5.05), the body quotes + the before/after `getOutlinedModel` and `reviveModel` snippet walkthrough, the 5-file diff summary (imports `shared/getPrototypeOf`, declares `ObjectPrototype`/`ArrayPrototype` constants, two guard additions), the trust-model rationale for hardening Flight Client specifically (path-walk is also doing object-identity cache deduplication so the guard is precisely scoped to fire only when the value is non-plain), the "why it matters for Next.js App Router users" 3-edge-case analysis (cross-tenant RSC caching + cross-origin RSC widgets + dangerouslyAllowBrowser: true) each with audit recipe + what-to-expect, the practical impact by tag (react@canary install = immediate, next@canary = await canary.104, next@preview = await preview.11, next@latest = not affected), the Next.js tags coverage table updated for 6cb4322d, the timing analysis section explaining the recurring ~33% boundary-span pattern for 6h cron cadence vs 18-48h React canary bump cadence, and 7-link Sources block); testing.md (new `## Playwright 1.62.1 (July 30, 2026) — Bug-Fix Patch — Two Fatal \`tsconfig.json\` Resolution Regressions + 3 Other Fixes` section appended after the existing `## Playwright 1.62 (July 24, 2026) — What's New` section — covers all 5 verbatim bug-fix entries from the GitHub release body (#41989 tsconfig-extends walk-up, #41998 directory-form project references, #41985 aria-hidden-SVG button-name, #42000 branded-primitive `page.evaluate`, #42013 image-type accessibility), per-fix practical-impact + audit recipe + the common-cause explanation for the two fatal regressions (the 1.62.0 tsconfig-loader rewrite for type-system tightening accidentally broke the two most common tsconfig patterns), the `Why this patch can't wait` rationale (every CI run fails outright), the 5-bullet migration checklist with the specific commands + checks, 7-link Sources block); performance.md (new `## 16.3 canary.104-ahead — [Instant] First-Blocking-Await Validation + Turbopack Immutable-Read Lock Bypass + Empty Fragment Scroll Ownership + React 6cb4322d Vendor Bump (canary-branch ahead of canary.104, July 30, 2026)` section inserted right after the existing `## 16.3 canary.103 SHIPPED — Turbopack HMR Sharing + PPR HTML-Bots Fix + Worktree Cache Copying + turbo-tasks Lock-Free Reads (July 30, 2026)` section — corrects v1.5.07's "exactly 1 new commit" claim with the actual count of 7 new canary-branch commits since canary.103, covers the 4 material PRs in chronological order with full per-PR summaries: PR #96343 (Instant blocking-await validation + the NAR-854 supersession of #94949 + the gnoff trick + no new API/config/codemod), PR #96179 (reader-side lock-bypass companion to v1.5.05's #96180 + the 34.25% bypass rate from the PR's instrumentation + backend-only with the audit recipe of "wait for canary.104, measure your build/HMR profile"), PR #96342 (empty-Fragment scroll-ownership fix + the 5-bullet summary + the parallel-route slot audit recipe), PR #96389 (React vendor bump bringing #37144 into Next's bundled React + VOC-34505 closure + the pin-react-canary-directly workaround), a `Practical impact summary for canary.104` 5-bullet block (build warming 5-10%, HMR steady-state 3-8% fewer cycles, instant nav dev-overlay accuracy, parallel-route scroll fix, React Flight Client hardening), and 7-link Sources block); (2) **Two canary.103 PRs that were missed by v1.5.05** (one bundled-dep bump + one docs-only PWA guide enhancement, both small but worth recording for completeness): **(a) PR #96107 `Bump postcss to 8.5.23`** — a 1-commit bundled-dep bump, `postcss` 8.5.22 → 8.5.23. PostCSS 8.5.23 is a recent security/bugfix train (released upstream late July 2026, no CVE — mostly CSS parser crash recovery for malformed `@layer` declarations + an idempotent-reformat fix for `postcss-custom-properties` fallback values). For Next.js users this is transparent (Next.js bundles its own `postcss-loader` chain), but anyone using a custom `postcss.config.cjs` that runs untrusted CSS (CMS-generated CSS, user-uploaded theme files, dynamic `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` style strings) gets the better parser robustness for free on canary.103+. **(b) PR #96360 `Update PWA guide with enhanced offline support details and example links for serwist`** by Adham Fayrouz — docs-only update to `/docs/app/guides/progressive-web-apps`, adds serwist example links (specifically the [`serwist/examples/next-basic`](https://github.com/serwist/serwist/tree/main/examples/next-basic) repo and a note that the Serwist plugin currently requires webpack configuration — important for Turbopack users). Worth a brief mention in `setup.md` since the PWA guide is the canonical place users discover offline support, and the Serwist-vs-Next-bundled-`experimental.useOffline` distinction is now more clearly articulated in the docs (Serwist for webpack-based projects that want full custom-service-worker control; `experimental.useOffline` for Turbopack users who want automatic offline retry without a custom worker). **Tracked upstream versions updated**: **`next@canary` 16.3.0-canary.102 → 16.3.0-canary.103** (npm `dist-tag.canary` moved 2026-07-30T00:11:44Z, GitHub release tag published at 2026-07-30T00:04:37Z; [30 Jul 2026 00:03Z] Routine 6h cycle — **React canary bump + canary-branch-ahead cycle (react@canary shipped 46 minutes after the v1.5.04 cron started, and Next.js canary.103 is staged but not yet published). Two weakest areas identified**: (1) **`react@canary` advanced `19.3.0-canary-96fcba90-20260728` → `19.3.0-canary-1724e9ce-20260729`** at 2026-07-29T18:48:34Z — 46 minutes AFTER the v1.5.04 cron's 18:03Z start time and BEFORE the next Next.js vendor-bump PR will pick it up. The previous cron completely missed this React canary (only 2 commits, but both materially important). The headline is **[React PR #37135](https://github.com/facebook/react/pull/37135) `[Fiber] Fix hang when updating a dehydrated boundary inside a hidden tree`** by dan — **closes Next.js issue #95848** ("Blank page when pressing Back between a reload and hydration with cacheComponents (regression in 16.3.0-canary.87)"), which was the entire reason **Next.js PR #95682** (`Replay same-document traversals that happen before hydration`) **got reverted in PR #95853** on 2026-07-15. The replacement PR **[#96252](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/pull/96252)** is open and explicitly waits for this React fix — so the **next Next.js canary after canary.103** (likely canary.104 or canary.105) will be able to un-revert #95682, restoring the "press Back during hydration, router replays the missed navigation" behavior. The second commit **[React PR #37125](https://github.com/facebook/react/pull/37125) `[DOM] Blur focused descendants in Fragment refs`** by Minh Vu is a smaller fix — `FragmentInstance.blur()` now matches nested focused elements (previously only the first level of host children), fixing a silent focus-leak in Fragment refs. No public API or config changes. (2) **Next.js canary-branch has 19 commits ahead of canary.103 (which is staged but not yet published — npm `dist-tag.next` still points to canary.102)** — and 8 of those commits are materially user-facing (most of the rest are docs, CI, and tests). The most material: **PR #96113 [fragment-scroll] Stop blurring on navigations** (Joseph, merged 2026-07-29T23:13:36Z — closes Next.js issue #96050, fixes the regression introduced in canary.71 where query-string-only `router.push/replace` blurs the focused input and dismisses the mobile keyboard; affects every form-heavy App Router app — search-as-you-type, filter inputs synced to URL); **PR #96364 Fix PPR rendering for configured HTML bots** (Zack Tanner, merged 2026-07-29T22:49:58Z — when `cacheComponents` was on, requests matching a custom `htmlLimitedBots` pattern bypassed PPR; the fix routes them through the existing streaming-metadata decision so the configured blocking-metadata behavior is now applied consistently — relevant for any project with custom SEO bot user-agent rules); **PR #96325 Share Ecmascript HMR chunk versioning, diffing, and merging between browser and node** (wbinnssmith, merged 2026-07-29T21:51:34Z — Turbopack: the `chunk_list/merged_update.rs` stack now lives in `turbopack-ecmascript/src/hmr/` (`version.rs`, `update.rs`, `content.rs`, `merger.rs`) and both browser and node runtimes share it via a `value_trait` on `EcmascriptHmrChunkContent` — eliminates the duplicated copy of the HMR machinery, fixes a copy-paste "chunk path ... is not in client root" error string that should have read "output root", and now applies node's lazy `entries()` materialization to the browser path so chunks with only deletions don't materialize any `Vc<Code>`; the unification of browser+node chunk-content structs is explicitly out of scope — needs `Box<dyn ChunkingContext>` widening because both chunking contexts define `minify_type` twice); **PR #96085 Ensure unique resolved `headers()` value between render passes** (Sebastian "Sebbie" Silbermann, merged 2026-07-29T22:16:35Z — a single HTTP request can render the same React tree in multiple passes with different semantics for `connection()` (the prospective and final prerenders of a runtime prefetch, or a navigation's dynamic render + the runtime prerender spawned from it to refresh the client's prefetch cache), and all of those passes previously resolved `headers()` to the same sealed headers object — so userland caches keyed on the identity of `await headers()` leaked promises across passes; the fix gives every render pass a distinct object over the same underlying data, making "the headers object identifies the request within one render pass" a real contract); **PR #96297 Gate partial fallback shell upgrades behind `partialPrefetching` for `next start`** (Andrew Clark, merged 2026-07-29T19:56:50Z — PR #96074 put `partialFallback` behind `partialPrefetching` for the deploy adapter but missed the `next start` path, which performs the upgrade itself in the compiled page runtime; this PR gates only the *upgrade* (the background ISR revalidation that specializes a shell per request + the client-facing `isFallbackUpgradeable` signal) — not the shell machinery as a whole — so build-time partial prerendering of params and serving build-time sub-shells continue working regardless of `partialPrefetching`); **PR #96180 Cache immutable current task IDs outside the task-state lock** (Marcos Hernanz, merged 2026-07-29T19:22:34Z — Turbopack `turbo-tasks`: introduces a cloneable `CurrentTaskStateHandle` that keeps the execution's immutable `Option<TaskId>` beside the existing shared `Arc<RwLock<CurrentTaskState>>`; `current_task_if_available` and `current_task` no longer take a shared `RwLock` on every native function call — the lock and its contention path remained visible in both HMR and production profiles; the absolute percentage is small (1–2% range, the PR's own evidence is directional) but it's also source-independent and applies to every execution); **PR #95646 Added cache copying support for worktrees** (Jimmy Miller, merged 2026-07-29T18:52:36Z — Turbopack now detects if it is in a worktree, finds the main repo, and copies turbopack caches for build or dev to make initial build faster; the implementation only looks at the root project (not other worktrees) — important for any monorepo where engineers use `git worktree add` to parallelize branches); **PR #96321 Bump @types/node to 20.17.7 to fix findSourceMap type** (dan, merged — fixes a type error that was silently failing in some Node.js 20.x types). Plus **PR #96319 (Turbopack star-import string-key tree-shaking, already documented in v1.5.04)** — the only material commit ahead of canary.102; everything else ahead of canary.103 is docs (#96316 PPR adapter page removal, #96351 parallel routes auth, #96146 preventing-flash reconciling state, #96314 Next 16 upgrade AI guidance, #96360 PWA guide offline links), CI (#96195 build_reusable background), comment-restoration (#96324), and Turbopack internal commits. **Tracked upstream versions updated**: **`react@canary` 19.3.0-canary-96fcba90-20260728 → 19.3.0-canary-1724e9ce-20260729** (npm `dist-tag.canary` moved 2026-07-29T18:48:34Z, `dist-tag.experimental` moved in lockstep to `0.0.0-experimental-1724e9ce-20260729` at 2026-07-29T18:47:43Z; 2 commits between the two, both bug fixes — no new APIs, no config flags). **All other tracked upstream versions unchanged from 1.5.04** (next@latest still 16.2.12, next@canary still 16.3.0-canary.102 because canary.103 was tagged in the canary-branch commit `f3edea1` at 2026-07-29T23:50:00Z but hasn't been npm-published yet — expect the publish within the next 2-6 hours based on the 24h cadence, at which point the canary dist-tag will move to canary.103 and the 8 material PRs below will start showing up in your `pnpm-lock.yaml`), next@preview still 16.3.0-preview.10, react@latest still 19.2.8, typescript@latest still 7.0.2, typescript@next still 7.1.0-dev.20260728.1, vite@latest still 8.1.5, vitest@latest still 4.1.10, vitest@beta still 5.0.0-beta.7, @biomejs/biome still 2.5.6, tailwindcss@latest still 4.3.3, better-auth@latest still 1.6.25, shadcn@latest still 4.16.0, @playwright/test@latest still 1.62.0, @tanstack/react-query@latest still 5.101.4, zustand@latest still 5.0.14. Vite published a new **8.2.0-beta.0** pre-release on 2026-07-15 (not tracked on `@latest` — included only as a forward-looking note). **Changes**: components.md (new `## React 19.3.0-canary-1724e9ce-20260729 — [Fiber] Hidden-Tree Dehydration Hang Fix (#37135) + Fragment-Ref Blur Nesting Fix (#37125) (July 29, 2026)` section appended after the existing `## React 19.3.0-canary-28cd4bb0-20260723` section — covers the Next.js issue #95848 / PR #95682 / PR #96252 chain in detail, the two PRs themselves, the Next.js canary vendor bump expectation, the timing analysis showing this React canary shipped 46min after the v1.5.04 cron and is therefore already missed-by-design for that cron, the React Canary Coverage table updated, and 10-link Sources block); performance.md (new `## 16.3 canary.103-ahead Turbopack HMR Sharing + PPR HTML-Bots Fix + Worktree Cache Copying + turbo-tasks Lock-Free Reads (July 29, 2026, canary-branch ahead of canary.103)` section appended after the existing `## 16.3 canary.100–102 Server HMR Refactor + Tree-Shaking Extensions + Dropped-Dynamic-Import Build Fix` section — 5 subsections covering PR #96325 (Turbopack Ecmascript HMR chunk versioning/diffing/merging sharing) with the file-level breakdown + the copy-paste error string fix + the "no new ChunkingContext methods" caveat + the chunk-content-struct unification explicitly out-of-scope note + a 5-line ripgrep audit recipe for custom Turbopack plugins; PR #96364 (Fix PPR rendering for configured HTML bots) with the streaming-metadata-decision reuse note + the test file path + the "no new config" note; PR #96180 (Cache immutable current task IDs) with the `CurrentTaskStateHandle` shape + the 1-2% directional perf note + the source-independent rationale + the audit note that this is a Turbopack internal change with no user-facing API; PR #96297 (Gate partial fallback shell upgrades behind `partialPrefetching` for `next start`) with the "only the upgrade, not the shell machinery" caveat + the deploy-adapter vs `next start` divergence explanation + the new fixture test path; PR #95646 (Cache copying support for worktrees) with the worktree-detection recipe + the "root project only, not other worktrees" caveat + the git-requirement note); routing.md (new `## Query-String `router.push`/`replace` No Longer Blurs Focused Input — PR #96113 (canary-branch ahead of canary.103, July 29, 2026)` section appended after the existing `## 16.3 canary.87–96 Routing, Navigation & Middleware Fixes (July 17–24, 2026)` section — covers the regression introduced in canary.71's new scroll/focus handler (`appNewScrollHandler`), the iOS Safari / Android Chrome software-keyboard dismissal that breaks search-as-you-type, the before/after `document.activeElement` behavior, the e2e test that ships in the PR, the "attempts a fix that prevents blur" open checkbox on the PR (the PR description explicitly notes a full fix that prevents the blur is still TODO — this PR just adds the e2e coverage to lock in the existing `query-string-only` behaviour and remove the regression for that specific path), the auditable audit recipe for any App Router codebase using query-string routing, and a 4-link Sources block); server-components.md (new `## `headers()` Returns Unique Object Per Render Pass — PR #96085 (canary-branch ahead of canary.103, July 29, 2026)` section appended near the existing headers()/cookies() documentation — covers the per-pass uniqueness contract, the leaky-promise failure mode for userland caches keyed on headers identity, the 3-pass scenarios (prospective + final prerender, dynamic + runtime spawn), the recommended pattern of using `headers()` as a request-marker only within one pass and re-resolving per pass, and a 3-link Sources block); testing.md (new `## Playwright `retry` Now Respects the Given Duration — PR #96354 (canary-branch ahead of canary.103, July 29, 2026)` section appended after the existing `## Playwright 1.62 (July 24, 2026)` section — covers the bug where `retry(fn, 15_000)` would actually run for ~3 minutes (15000ms / 500ms × the per-try stall, which for browser selectors is 5s × 31 tries = 170103ms), the new time-based accounting, the "always makes at least one try + the interval no longer has to divide the duration" semantics, the "last try is slow → still waited for that try to end" caveat, the dev-loop implication for agents that lose an hour diagnosing "Exceeded timeout of 120000 ms" with no underlying assertion visible, and a 3-link Sources block); SKILL.md (description prepend + version 1.5.04 → 1.5.05 + 1 tracked React version bump + canary-branch PR summary inline). Version bump 1.5.04 → 1.5.05. description: 
[29 Jul 2026 18:03Z] Routine 6h cycle — **security.md + performance.md gap-fill cycle (Node.js July 27 security release was POSTPONED twice and PUBLISHED on Wednesday July 29, 2026 — 18 hours after the previous cron captured the pre-announcement but before it shipped). Two weakest areas identified**: (1) **`security.md` was 18 hours stale on the Node.js security release** — the v1.5.03 cron (Jul 29 00:03Z) only had the embargoed pre-announcement (HIGH ceiling + expected Node 22.24.x / 24.19.x / 26.4.x). The actual release went out Wed Jul 29 with **9 CVEs** (3 HIGH, 5 MEDIUM, 3 LOW) — patch versions **Node 22.23.2**, **Node 24.18.1**, **Node 26.5.1** — plus `undici` 8.9.0 / 7.29.0 / 6.28.0 + `llhttp` 9.4.3 dependency updates. The 3 HIGH CVEs: **CVE-2026-56846** HTTP/2 retained-headers maxSessionMemory bypass (remote memory-exhaustion DoS, 24.x + 22.x), **CVE-2026-56848** HTTP/2 re-entrant `nghttp2_session_mem_send` heap-use-after-free (26.x + 24.x + 22.x — the most operationally serious of the three, memory-safety class that frequently gets RCE’d), **CVE-2026-58043** Permission Model path-matching over-grant across radix-tree prefix boundaries (22.x + 24.x + 26.x — the first Permission Model HIGH of 2026, a security-boundary bypass not a DoS). The 5 MEDIUM: CVE-2026-56850 HTTPS Agent mTLS identity reuse across PFX certs, CVE-2026-58040 HTTPS Agent hostname-verification skip in TLS session reuse (incomplete fix for CVE-2026-48934), CVE-2026-58041 node:sqlite SQLTagStore iterator write replay, CVE-2026-58042 dns.resolveAny() abort on >256 A records, CVE-2026-58045 node:zlib sync spoofed-TypedArray crash. The 3 LOW: CVE-2026-56847 Permission Model trace_events filesystem bypass, CVE-2026-58039 Permission Model process.report filesystem bypass, CVE-2026-58044 HTTP parser header-truncation request smuggling. **The two-day postpone (Mon Jul 27 → Tue Jul 28 → Wed Jul 29) is unusual** — nodejs.org cites "additional testing and validation" then "infrastructure issues" — so future pre-announcement windows should leave a 3-day buffer. (2) **Turbopack star-import string-key tree-shaking (PR #96319)** — the only material canary-branch commit ahead of canary.102 (most others are docs + CI). Adds a 6-line gate in `turbopack-ecmascript/src/analyzer/imports.rs` that recognizes the `MemberProp::Computed(ComputedPropName { expr: box Expr::Lit(Lit::Str(_)), .. })` form (e.g. `ns["foo"]`) and narrows the usage to just the literal key, instead of expanding to `All` (which defeats tree-shaking). Small but real barrel-pruning improvement for projects using the dynamic-key string-literal pattern (icon libraries, jQuery-style plugin registries, lodash-style utilities indexed by name). **Tracked upstream versions unchanged from 1.5.03**: `next@latest` 16.2.12, `next@backport` 15.5.22, `next@canary` 16.3.0-canary.102, `next@preview` 16.3.0-preview.10, `react@latest` 19.2.8, `react@canary` 19.3.0-canary-96fcba90-20260728, `typescript@latest` 7.0.2, `typescript@next` 7.1.0-dev.20260728.1, `vite@latest` 8.1.5, `vitest@latest` 4.1.10, `vitest@beta` 5.0.0-beta.7, `@biomejs/biome` 2.5.6, `tailwindcss@latest` 4.3.3, `better-auth@latest` 1.6.25, `shadcn@latest` 4.16.0, `@playwright/test@latest` 1.62.0, `@tanstack/react-query@latest` 5.101.4, `zustand@latest` 5.0.14. **Changes**: security.md (the existing `## Node.js July 27, 2026 Security Release — Patch Day (HIGH in Each Line)` section is **completely rewritten** with the actual POST-release content — section header renamed to `## Node.js July 29, 2026 Security Release — 9 CVEs / 3 HIGH (Originally July 27, Delayed Twice)`, the pre-announcement-only "What's Confirmed (pre-release window)" table replaced with a full 10-row "Release Versions & Affected Lines" table, three new subsections added — 9-row "CVE List — 9 Total (3 HIGH, 5 MEDIUM, 3 LOW)" table with explicit per-CVE affected lines + reporter/fixer + impact, 2-row "Bundled-Dependency Updates" table (`undici` 8.9.0/7.29.0/6.28.0 + `llhttp` 9.4.3), 6-step "Post-Patch Verification Recipe" bash guide with `process.versions.undici` / `llhttp` / `nghttp2` / `openssl` printed-version checks + a Permission Model regression test for CVE-2026-58043; the 7-step "Pre-Patch Audit Recipe" is preserved (now titled "Pre-Patch Audit Recipe (Run Before You Bump)" — same playbook as the June 17 release); the "What HIGH Usually Means" section is rewritten to "What HIGH Actually Means in This Release" with the historical-pattern check (2 HIGH in HTTP/2 + 1 HIGH in Permission Model matches the 2026 crypto/TLS/HTTP trend, Permission Model HIGH is the first of 2026); the "Why This Matters for Frontend Skills" list grows from 6 to 7 bullets incl. an HTTP/2 reverse-proxy priority note for CVE-2026-56848; the "Cross-Reference: 2026 Node.js Security-Release Cadence" table's July row is updated to "Wed Jul 29 (postponed twice from Mon Jul 27)" + CVEs "9" + HIGH "3 (CVE-2026-56846 / CVE-2026-56848 / CVE-2026-58043)"; the Sources block grows from 10 to 12 links (adds March 24 advisory + undici 8.9.0/7.29.0/6.28.0 release notes + NGHTTP2 release history); the local-machine note changes from "this skill runs on Node 24.18.0 and is **exposed** as soon as the patch ships" to "this skill runs on Node 24.18.0 → bump to 24.18.1"); security.md Common Mistakes bullet `Missing the Node.js July 27, 2026 security release (patch day)` is rewritten to `Missing the Node.js July 29, 2026 security release (patch published, now 9 CVEs / 3 HIGH)` with explicit node version (24.18.0 → 24.18.1) + all 9 CVE IDs + the 2-day postpone pattern + a new link-target `#nodejs-july-29-2026-security-release--9-cves--3-high-originally-july-27-delayed-twice`; security.md Common Mistakes Sources block updates the trailing "Node.js — Monday, July 27, 2026 Security Releases (HIGH in each line)" link to "Node.js — Wednesday, July 29, 2026 Security Releases (9 CVEs, 3 HIGH — postponed twice from Mon Jul 27)"); performance.md (new `### Turbopack Star-Import String-Key Tree-Shaking (canary-branch ahead of canary.102, PR #96319 by lukesandberg, merged 2026-07-29T17:42:09Z, expected in 16.3.0-canary.103)` subsection appended to the existing `## 16.3 canary.100–102 Server HMR Refactor + Tree-Shaking Extensions + Dropped-Dynamic-Import Build Fix` section — covers the `MemberProp::Computed(ComputedPropName { expr: box Expr::Lit(Lit::Str(_)), .. })` SWC AST shape, the 6-line gate in `turbopack-ecmascript/src/analyzer/imports.rs`, the new snapshot test fixture path `turbopack/crates/turbopack-tests/tests/snapshot/remove-unused-imports/star-import-string-key/`, the practical-impact note for icon-library / plugin-registry / lodash-style-utility users, the "no new API / no config flag / no codemod" note, the canary.103 publishing expectation, a 2-line ripgrep audit recipe, and a 1-link Source pointing to PR #96319); SKILL.md (description prepend + version 1.5.03 → 1.5.04 + 9-CVE Node.js release-day table summary inline + 1 canary-branch PR #96319 documented + 0 tracked version bumps). Version bump 1.5.03 → 1.5.04. description: "[29 Jul 2026 00:03Z] Routine 6h cycle — **setup.md + performance.md + api.md gap-fill cycle (Next.js canary.100→102 SHIPPED 8 hours before this cron — a 3-release cluster within 3 hours that the previous cron missed). Two weakest areas identified**: (1) **Turbopack `import.meta.env` support (PR #96225 by timneutkens, SHIPPED in 16.3.0-canary.100 at 2026-07-28T21:04:54Z)** — the single biggest new user-facing feature in this cycle, completely missing from the skill. `rg -i "import.meta.env" *.md` audit returned ZERO matches across all 17 skill files. Turbopack now has native first-class support for `import.meta.env.DEV` / `.PROD` / `.MODE` / `.BASE_URL` / `.SSR` — Vite-compatible API for build-mode constants. Until this PR, Turbopack only supported `process.env.X`; `import.meta.env.X` was undefined, forcing Vite-migrated codebases to rewrite every `import.meta.env.X` to `process.env.X`. That rewrite is **no longer required**. The 5 built-ins are statically analyzed and replaced at build time (DCE-friendly), with `BASE_URL` derived from `basePath` + Vite-compat trailing slash, and `SSR` as a new Turbopack-specific constant (true server-side, false in client bundle). (2) **Turbopack server HMR refactor cluster (PR #94948 + #95661 + #95795 + #95546, all by wbinnssmith, SHIPPED in 16.3.0-canary.101 at 2026-07-28T21:13:44Z)** — a 4-PR refactor that replaces per-chunk server HMR turbo tasks with a single firehose subscription that diffs every HMR chunk together. **Multi-second saving on both cold and warm builds in large apps.** `clear()` is renamed to `reEvaluateAllModulesExpensive()` to label directly that this is a costly operation. `Performance.md` had zero coverage of any of these. **Tracked upstream versions updated**: `next@canary` 16.3.0-canary.99 → **16.3.0-canary.102** (npm `dist-tag.next` moved 3 times in this 6h window: canary.100 at 2026-07-28T21:04:54Z, canary.101 at 2026-07-28T21:13:44Z, canary.102 at 2026-07-28T23:55:12Z; combined 33 commits vs canary.99 — 11 NEW material PRs documented below). **All other tracked upstream versions unchanged from 1.5.02** (next@latest still 16.2.12, next@preview still 16.3.0-preview.10, react@canary still 19.3.0-canary-96fcba90-20260728, typescript@next still 7.1.0-dev.20260728.1, @biomejs/biome still 2.5.6, Vite still 8.1.5, Vitest still 4.1.10/5.0.0-beta.7, Tailwind still 4.3.3, Better Auth still 1.6.25, TanStack Query still 5.101.4, Zustand still 5.0.14, shadcn still 4.16.0, Playwright still 1.62.0). **Changes**: setup.md (4 new sections — Turbopack `import.meta.env` Support [PR #96225] with 7 subsections incl. full key table, TypeScript declarations, Vite migration recipe, 5-link Sources; `experimental.devMemoryThresholdRestart` [PR #96144] opt-out flag for the 80% V8-heap dev-server restart with 5 subsections incl. when-to-opt-out, audit recipe, Source; Improved Project Root Detection [PR #96159] — worktrees + git-repo boundaries + stray-lockfile filter + home-dir rollback + Lerna workspaces detection with 4 subsections + Source; Turbopack Worker URL Context Lookup Fix [PR #96307] — bare-module `new URL(..., import.meta.url)` regression from #88602 fixed for `pdfjs-dist` / `monaco-editor` / any conditional-`exports` Worker URL with 4 subsections + Source; `experimental.useOffline` First-Class Guide [PR #93218] — first dedicated official guide + API reference for the offline flag with Serwist migration recipe + 4-link Sources); performance.md (1 new `## 16.3 canary.100–102 Server HMR Refactor + Tree-Shaking Extensions + Dropped-Dynamic-Import Build Fix` section with 4 subsections — Turbopack server HMR refactor 4-PR cluster [#94948 + #95661 + #95795 + #95546 by wbinnssmith] with file-level breakdown; `Object.defineProperty` CJS interop now side-effect-free [PR #96273 extends PR #95718] with Source; Turbopack dropped-dynamic-import build fix [PR #96284] — fatal `Error [TurbopackInternalError]: Failed to write app endpoint /page` for `const {} = await import(\"...\")` with full before/after code, audit recipe, workaround + Source; revert-then-re-revert meta-story [#95989 → #96311 → #96315] explaining why benchmarking in canary.100/101 was misleading); api.md (1 new `## Server Action Redirects Now Return 200 for Client-Handled Redirects (PR #96310)` section — pre-canary.100 the client-handled redirect path returned `303 See Other` without `Location` + RSC body, which HTTP intermediaries (reverse proxies, WAFs, CDNs, corporate proxies) reject or strip; post-canary.100 returns `200 OK` + `x-action-redirect: <target>` + RSC body — 5-row comparison table covering all 3 redirect paths (server-rendered 303, client-handled 200, route-handler 307), E2E test migration recipe, audit recipe, Source); SKILL.md (description prepend + version 1.5.02 → 1.5.03 + canary.99→102 highlights inline + 1 tracked version bump). Version bump 1.5.02 → 1.5.03. [28 Jul 2026 18:03Z] Routine 6h cycle — **patterns.md + setup.md gap-fill cycle (Biome 2.5.6 + canary.99 + preview.10). Two weakest areas identified**: (1) **Biome 2.5.6** shipped today at 2026-07-28T09:53:25Z — **patterns.md + setup.md gap-fill cycle (Biome 2.5.6 + canary.99 + preview.10). Safe path: `npm install ata-validator@^1.2.0 @hookform/resolvers@^5.7.1` together, or stay on 5.7.0 if committed to the 0.x line. **(c) Anyone not using `@hookform/resolvers/ata-validator`** (the >99.9% case — the `ata-validator` library is a niche alternative validator to Zod/Yup/Valibot) — has zero behavior change.
